input = None, capture_output = True, timeout = None, check = True
popenargs = (['bash', '<repo>/betrieb/einrichten.sh', '--erzeugen'],)
kwargs = {'env': {'COLORTERM': 'truecolor', 'HERDR_BIN_PATH': '~/.local/bin/herdr', 'HERDR_ENV': '1', 'HERDR_PANE_ID': 'wB:p1', ...}, 'stdout': -1, 'stderr': -1}

    def run(*popenargs,
            input=None, capture_output=False, timeout=None, check=False, **kwargs):
        """Run command with arguments and return a CompletedProcess instance.
    
        The returned instance will have attributes args, returncode, stdout and
        stderr. By default, stdout and stderr are not captured, and those attributes
        will be None. Pass stdout=PIPE and/or stderr=PIPE in order to capture them,
        or pass capture_output=True to capture both.
    
        If check is True and the exit code was non-zero, it raises a
        CalledProcessError. The CalledProcessError object will have the return code
        in the returncode attribute, and output & stderr attributes if those streams
        were captured.
    
        If timeout (seconds) is given and the process takes too long,
         a TimeoutExpired exception will be raised.
    
        There is an optional argument "input", allowing you to
        pass bytes or a string to the subprocess's stdin.  If you use this argument
        you may not also use the Popen constructor's "stdin" argument, as
        it will be used internally.
    
        By default, all communication is in bytes, and therefore any "input" should
        be bytes, and the stdout and stderr will be bytes. If in text mode, any
        "input" should be a string, and stdout and stderr will be strings decoded
        according to locale encoding, or by "encoding" if set. Text mode is
        triggered by setting any of text, encoding, errors or universal_newlines.
    
        The other arguments are the same as for the Popen constructor.
        """
        if input is not None:
            if kwargs.get('stdin') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdin and input arguments may not both be used.')
            kwargs['stdin'] = PIPE
    
        if capture_output:
            if kwargs.get('stdout') is not None or kwargs.get('stderr') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdout and stderr arguments may not be used '
                                 'with capture_output.')
            kwargs['stdout'] = PIPE
            kwargs['stderr'] = PIPE
    
        with Popen(*popenargs, **kwargs) as process:
            try:
                stdout, stderr = process.communicate(input, timeout=timeout)
            except TimeoutExpired as exc:
                process.kill()
                if _mswindows:
                    # Windows accumulates the output in a single blocking
                    # read() call run on child threads, with the timeout
                    # being done in a join() on those threads.  communicate()
                    # _after_ kill() is required to collect that and add it
                    # to the exception.
                    exc.stdout, exc.stderr = process.communicate()
                else:
                    # POSIX _communicate already populated the output so
                    # far into the TimeoutExpired exception.
                    process.wait()
                raise
            except:  # Including KeyboardInterrupt, communicate handled that.
                process.kill()
                # We don't call process.wait() as .__exit__ does that for us.
                raise
            retcode = process.poll()
            if check and retcode:
>               raise CalledProcessError(retcode, process.args,
                                         output=stdout, stderr=stderr)
E               subprocess.CalledProcessError: Command '['bash', '<repo>/betrieb/einrichten.sh', '--erzeugen']' returned non-zero exit status 1.

    def run(*popenargs,
            input=None, capture_output=False, timeout=None, check=False, **kwargs):
        """Run command with arguments and return a CompletedProcess instance.
    
        The returned instance will have attributes args, returncode, stdout and
        stderr. By default, stdout and stderr are not captured, and those attributes
        will be None. Pass stdout=PIPE and/or stderr=PIPE in order to capture them,
        or pass capture_output=True to capture both.
    
        If check is True and the exit code was non-zero, it raises a
        CalledProcessError. The CalledProcessError object will have the return code
        in the returncode attribute, and output & stderr attributes if those streams
        were captured.
    
        If timeout (seconds) is given and the process takes too long,
         a TimeoutExpired exception will be raised.
    
        There is an optional argument "input", allowing you to
        pass bytes or a string to the subprocess's stdin.  If you use this argument
        you may not also use the Popen constructor's "stdin" argument, as
        it will be used internally.
    
        By default, all communication is in bytes, and therefore any "input" should
        be bytes, and the stdout and stderr will be bytes. If in text mode, any
        "input" should be a string, and stdout and stderr will be strings decoded
        according to locale encoding, or by "encoding" if set. Text mode is
        triggered by setting any of text, encoding, errors or universal_newlines.
    
        The other arguments are the same as for the Popen constructor.
        """
        if input is not None:
            if kwargs.get('stdin') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdin and input arguments may not both be used.')
            kwargs['stdin'] = PIPE
    
        if capture_output:
            if kwargs.get('stdout') is not None or kwargs.get('stderr') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdout and stderr arguments may not be used '
                                 'with capture_output.')
            kwargs['stdout'] = PIPE
            kwargs['stderr'] = PIPE
    
        with Popen(*popenargs, **kwargs) as process:
            try:
                stdout, stderr = process.communicate(input, timeout=timeout)
            except TimeoutExpired as exc:
                process.kill()
                if _mswindows:
                    # Windows accumulates the output in a single blocking
                    # read() call run on child threads, with the timeout
                    # being done in a join() on those threads.  communicate()
                    # _after_ kill() is required to collect that and add it
                    # to the exception.
                    exc.stdout, exc.stderr = process.communicate()
                else:
                    # POSIX _communicate already populated the output so
                    # far into the TimeoutExpired exception.
                    process.wait()
                raise
            except:  # Including KeyboardInterrupt, communicate handled that.
                process.kill()
                # We don't call process.wait() as .__exit__ does that for us.
                raise
            retcode = process.poll()

input = None, capture_output = True, timeout = None, check = True
popenargs = (['bash', '<repo>/betrieb/einrichten.sh', '--erzeugen'],)
kwargs = {'env': {'COLORTERM': 'truecolor', 'HERDR_BIN_PATH': '~/.local/bin/herdr', 'HERDR_ENV': '1', 'HERDR_PANE_ID': 'wB:p1', ...}, 'stdout': -1, 'stderr': -1}

    def run(*popenargs,
            input=None, capture_output=False, timeout=None, check=False, **kwargs):
        """Run command with arguments and return a CompletedProcess instance.
    
        The returned instance will have attributes args, returncode, stdout and
        stderr. By default, stdout and stderr are not captured, and those attributes
        will be None. Pass stdout=PIPE and/or stderr=PIPE in order to capture them,
        or pass capture_output=True to capture both.
    
        If check is True and the exit code was non-zero, it raises a
        CalledProcessError. The CalledProcessError object will have the return code
        in the returncode attribute, and output & stderr attributes if those streams
        were captured.
    
        If timeout (seconds) is given and the process takes too long,
         a TimeoutExpired exception will be raised.
    
        There is an optional argument "input", allowing you to
        pass bytes or a string to the subprocess's stdin.  If you use this argument
        you may not also use the Popen constructor's "stdin" argument, as
        it will be used internally.
    
        By default, all communication is in bytes, and therefore any "input" should
        be bytes, and the stdout and stderr will be bytes. If in text mode, any
        "input" should be a string, and stdout and stderr will be strings decoded
        according to locale encoding, or by "encoding" if set. Text mode is
        triggered by setting any of text, encoding, errors or universal_newlines.
    
        The other arguments are the same as for the Popen constructor.
        """
        if input is not None:
            if kwargs.get('stdin') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdin and input arguments may not both be used.')
            kwargs['stdin'] = PIPE
    
        if capture_output:
            if kwargs.get('stdout') is not None or kwargs.get('stderr') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdout and stderr arguments may not be used '
                                 'with capture_output.')
            kwargs['stdout'] = PIPE
            kwargs['stderr'] = PIPE
    
        with Popen(*popenargs, **kwargs) as process:
            try:
                stdout, stderr = process.communicate(input, timeout=timeout)
            except TimeoutExpired as exc:
                process.kill()
                if _mswindows:
                    # Windows accumulates the output in a single blocking
                    # read() call run on child threads, with the timeout
                    # being done in a join() on those threads.  communicate()
                    # _after_ kill() is required to collect that and add it
                    # to the exception.
                    exc.stdout, exc.stderr = process.communicate()
                else:
                    # POSIX _communicate already populated the output so
                    # far into the TimeoutExpired exception.
                    process.wait()
                raise
            except:  # Including KeyboardInterrupt, communicate handled that.
                process.kill()
                # We don't call process.wait() as .__exit__ does that for us.
                raise
            retcode = process.poll()
            if check and retcode:
>               raise CalledProcessError(retcode, process.args,
                                         output=stdout, stderr=stderr)
E               subprocess.CalledProcessError: Command '['bash', '<repo>/betrieb/einrichten.sh', '--erzeugen']' returned non-zero exit status 1.

input = None, capture_output = True, timeout = None, check = True
popenargs = (['bash', '<repo>/betrieb/einrichten.sh', '--erzeugen'],)
kwargs = {'env': {'COLORTERM': 'truecolor', 'HERDR_BIN_PATH': '~/.local/bin/herdr', 'HERDR_ENV': '1', 'HERDR_PANE_ID': 'wB:p1', ...}, 'stdout': -1, 'stderr': -1}

    def run(*popenargs,
            input=None, capture_output=False, timeout=None, check=False, **kwargs):
        """Run command with arguments and return a CompletedProcess instance.
    
        The returned instance will have attributes args, returncode, stdout and
        stderr. By default, stdout and stderr are not captured, and those attributes
        will be None. Pass stdout=PIPE and/or stderr=PIPE in order to capture them,
        or pass capture_output=True to capture both.
    
        If check is True and the exit code was non-zero, it raises a
        CalledProcessError. The CalledProcessError object will have the return code
        in the returncode attribute, and output & stderr attributes if those streams
        were captured.
    
        If timeout (seconds) is given and the process takes too long,
         a TimeoutExpired exception will be raised.
    
        There is an optional argument "input", allowing you to
        pass bytes or a string to the subprocess's stdin.  If you use this argument
        you may not also use the Popen constructor's "stdin" argument, as
        it will be used internally.
    
        By default, all communication is in bytes, and therefore any "input" should
        be bytes, and the stdout and stderr will be bytes. If in text mode, any
        "input" should be a string, and stdout and stderr will be strings decoded
        according to locale encoding, or by "encoding" if set. Text mode is
        triggered by setting any of text, encoding, errors or universal_newlines.
    
        The other arguments are the same as for the Popen constructor.
        """
        if input is not None:
            if kwargs.get('stdin') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdin and input arguments may not both be used.')
            kwargs['stdin'] = PIPE
    
        if capture_output:
            if kwargs.get('stdout') is not None or kwargs.get('stderr') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdout and stderr arguments may not be used '
                                 'with capture_output.')
            kwargs['stdout'] = PIPE
            kwargs['stderr'] = PIPE
    
        with Popen(*popenargs, **kwargs) as process:
            try:
                stdout, stderr = process.communicate(input, timeout=timeout)
            except TimeoutExpired as exc:
                process.kill()
                if _mswindows:
                    # Windows accumulates the output in a single blocking
                    # read() call run on child threads, with the timeout
                    # being done in a join() on those threads.  communicate()
                    # _after_ kill() is required to collect that and add it
                    # to the exception.
                    exc.stdout, exc.stderr = process.communicate()
                else:
                    # POSIX _communicate already populated the output so
                    # far into the TimeoutExpired exception.
                    process.wait()
                raise
            except:  # Including KeyboardInterrupt, communicate handled that.
                process.kill()
                # We don't call process.wait() as .__exit__ does that for us.
                raise
            retcode = process.poll()
            if check and retcode:
>               raise CalledProcessError(retcode, process.args,
                                         output=stdout, stderr=stderr)
E               subprocess.CalledProcessError: Command '['bash', '<repo>/betrieb/einrichten.sh', '--erzeugen']' returned non-zero exit status 1.

        vor, scheitert in deploy.sh jedes sudo. Die fälligen Neustarts werden dann in <FR_DIENST>/neustart-ausstehend

        class Gesund(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
            def do_GET(self):
                self.send_response(200)
                self.end_headers()
                self.wfile.write(b"ok")
    
            def log_message(self, *args):
                pass
        server = ThreadingHTTPServer(("127.0.0.1", 0), Gesund)
        threading.Thread(target=server.serve_forever, daemon=True).start()

    def test_einrichten_installieren_attrappe(tmp_path):
        """AC-20 (Installationsweg): --installieren gegen eine sudo-Attrappe installiert Reise-Helfer, Units und eine sudoers-Datei
        nur mit Helfern; kein usermod -aG docker für den Dienstbenutzer. Ohne Netz: der Klon geht an eine git-Attrappe."""
        repo = tmp_path / "repo"
        (repo / "betrieb").mkdir(parents=True)
        (repo / "betrieb/einstellungen.env").write_text("FR_INSTANZ=probe\nFR_TITEL=Probe\nFR_ADRESSE=https://example.invalid\n")
        (repo / "engine").symlink_to(ENGINE)
        origin = tmp_path / "origin.git"
        subprocess.run(["git", "init", "-q", "--bare", str(origin)], check=True)
        subprocess.run(["git", "init", "-q", str(repo)], check=True)
        subprocess.run(["git", "-C", str(repo), "remote", "add", "origin", str(origin)], check=True)
        # Software-Klon (#454): einrichten.sh prüft, dass „git fetch origin main“ ohne Rückfrage geht
        subprocess.run(["git", "-C", str(repo), "-c", "user.name=Probe", "-c", "user.email=probe@example.invalid", "commit", "-q",
                        "--allow-empty", "-m", "Probe"], check=True)
        subprocess.run(["git", "-C", str(repo), "push", "-q", "origin", "HEAD:main"], check=True)
        bin_ = tmp_path / "bin"
        bin_.mkdir()
        log = tmp_path / "sudo.log"
        ausfuehrbar(bin_ / "sudo", f'#!/bin/sh\nprintf "%s\\n" "$*" >> {log}\nexit 0\n')
        ausfuehrbar(bin_ / "gh", "#!/bin/sh\nexit 0\n")
        git_ohne_klonen(bin_, log)
        for name in ("rsync", "flock"):
            if not shutil.which(name):

    def test_deploy_mit_warnung_im_plan_legt_die_seite_ab(tmp_path):

        # Rückmeldedienst-Attrappe: antwortet auf …/gesund, damit die Bereitschaftsprüfung am Ende von deploy.sh besteht
        import threading
        from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer
    
        class Gesund(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
            def do_GET(self):
                self.send_response(200)
                self.end_headers()
                self.wfile.write(b"ok")
    
            def log_message(self, *args):
                pass
        server = ThreadingHTTPServer(("127.0.0.1", 0), Gesund)
        threading.Thread(target=server.serve_forever, daemon=True).start()
        env = {**SAUBER, "FR_WURZEL": str(wurzel), "FR_INSTANZ": "probe", "FR_SEITEN": str(tmp_path / "seiten"),
               "FR_DIENST": str(tmp_path / "dienst"), "FR_PYTHON": sys.executable, "FR_GEPRUEFT": git(wurzel, "rev-parse", "HEAD").strip(),
               "FR_RM_PORT": str(server.server_address[1]), "FR_SUDO": "false"}
        try:
            r = subprocess.run([str(BETRIEB / "deploy.sh"), "beispielreise"], env=env, capture_output=True, text=True)
        finally:
            server.shutdown()

    def test_deploy_prueft_die_wege_zum_konnektor_von_aussen(tmp_path):
        """Nach dem Deploy fragt deploy.sh jede Art Pfad des Konnektors über die öffentliche Adresse ab. Kommt ein Weg bei
        nginx statt beim Konnektor an (kein Kopf X-Frieda), warnt es im Protokoll (ohne Meldung, #445); die Seite wird trotzdem
        abgelegt. So lief das Kalender-Abo ins Leere: nginx kannte /kalender/ nicht (1.11.2026)."""
        import threading
        from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer
        wurzel = tmp_path / "instanz"
        shutil.copytree(BEISPIEL, tmp_path / "dienst" / "bau" / "reisen" / "beispielreise")
        wurzel.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        (wurzel / "engine").symlink_to(ENGINE)
        (wurzel / ".gitignore").write_text("dist/\n__pycache__/\n")
        git(wurzel, "init", "-q", "-b", "main")
        git(wurzel, "add", ".")
        git(wurzel, "commit", "-qm", "start")
        (tmp_path / "seiten").mkdir()
        fehlt = {"/kalender/"}
    
        class Dienste(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
            """Rückmeldedienst, Konnektor und nginx davor in einem: X-Frieda nur, wo nginx zum Konnektor weiterleitet."""
            def do_GET(self):
                nginx_404 = any(self.path.startswith(f) for f in fehlt)
                self.send_response(404 if nginx_404 else 200)
                if not nginx_404:
                    self.send_header("X-Frieda", "konnektor")
                self.send_header("Content-Length", "0")
                self.end_headers()
            do_HEAD = do_GET
    
            def log_message(self, *args):
                pass
        server = ThreadingHTTPServer(("127.0.0.1", 0), Dienste)
        threading.Thread(target=server.serve_forever, daemon=True).start()
        port = str(server.server_address[1])
        bin_ = tmp_path / "bin"
        bin_.mkdir()
        ausfuehrbar(bin_ / "systemctl", '#!/bin/sh\ncase "$*" in *konnektor*) exit 0;; esac\nexit 1\n')
        meldungen = tmp_path / "meldungen.log"
        melden = ausfuehrbar(tmp_path / "melden.sh", f'#!/bin/sh\necho "$1|$2|$3" >> {meldungen}\n')
        env = {**SAUBER, "PATH": f"{bin_}:{SAUBER.get('PATH', os.environ['PATH'])}", "FR_WURZEL": str(wurzel), "FR_INSTANZ": "probe",
               "FR_SEITEN": str(tmp_path / "seiten"), "FR_DIENST": str(tmp_path / "dienst"), "FR_KONNEKTOR": str(tmp_path / "kn"),
               "FR_KONTEN": str(tmp_path / "konten"), "FR_PYTHON": sys.executable, "FR_GEPRUEFT": git(wurzel, "rev-parse", "HEAD").strip(),
               "FR_RM_PORT": port, "FR_KN_PORT": port, "FR_KN_ADRESSE": f"http://127.0.0.1:{port}", "FR_SUDO": "false",
               "FR_MELDEN": str(melden)}
        try:
            r = subprocess.run([str(BETRIEB / "deploy.sh"), "beispielreise"], env=env, capture_output=True, text=True)
>           assert r.returncode == 0, r.stdout + r.stderr
E           AssertionError: Done: /private/var/folders/9n/w5wl_q1x7j17v9y0b5nw6vj80000gn/T/pytest-of-<benutzer>/pytest-2713/popen-gw0/test_deploy_prueft_die_wege_zu0/instanz/dist/beispielreise/index.html (17 sections, 4 stages)

    def test_reise_baustein_ueber_helfer(tmp_path):

        ruft den Reise-Helfer (FR_NGINX_REISE_HELFER, davor FR_SUDO) mit dem Reisenamen auf stdin; dessen Ausgabe ist der Pfad.
        Link und seiten.json entstehen wie bisher."""

        snippets = tmp_path / "snippets"
        snippets.mkdir()

        helfer = tmp_path / "helfer.sh"
        helfer.write_text(f'#!/bin/sh\nread -r reise\necho "$# $* $reise" >> {aufrufe}\necho "$reise-0123456789abcdef01234567"\n'
                          f'[ -e {tmp_path}/schon-da ] && exit 2\ntouch {tmp_path}/schon-da\nexit 0\n')

        abruf.write_text("#!/bin/sh\necho 200\n")

        repo = tmp_path / "repo"

        env = {**SAUBER, "FR_WURZEL": str(repo), "FR_REISENORDNER": str(repo / "reisen"), "FR_INSTANZ": "probe", "FR_SEITEN": str(tmp_path / "seiten"),

               "FR_NGINX_REISE_HELFER": str(helfer), "FS_ABRUF": str(abruf), "FS_NGINX_TEST": "false", "FS_NGINX_LADEN": "false", "FS_WARTEN": "0", "FS_429_WARTEN": "0"}

    def test_units_ohne_handschritt(tmp_path):
        """AC-21: Die neue Vorlage hat keinen Pfad auf -schreiben mehr. Geändert vom Developer (#557): ReadWritePaths steht
        nicht in der Erlaubnisliste (units.py, Hilfsprogramm mit Root-Rechten), und die Spec verlangt sie unverändert. Eine
        installierte Unit mit der alten Zeile ist deshalb eine gesperrte Abweichung (Exit 4, BLOCKED mit ReadWritePaths), nie
        eine stille Installation; der Abgleich über units.py meldet sie, bis die Units einmal mit einrichten.sh --installieren
        aus dem Klon installiert sind (wie bei #454). Exit 3 wäre nur mit einer neuen Erlaubnis für ReadWritePaths möglich."""
        repo = tmp_path / "repo"

        (tmp_path / "dienst" / "instanz").mkdir(parents=True)
        (tmp_path / "dienst" / "instanz" / "einstellungen.env").write_text(
            "FR_INSTANZ=probe\nFR_TITEL=Probe\nFR_ADRESSE=https://example.invalid\n")

engine/tests/test_units.py:80: 
_ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ 

input = None, capture_output = False, timeout = None, check = True
popenargs = (['bash', '<repo>/betrieb/einrichten.sh', '--erzeugen'],)
kwargs = {'env': {'COLORTERM': 'truecolor', 'HERDR_BIN_PATH': '~/.local/bin/herdr', 'HERDR_ENV': '1', 'HERDR_PANE_ID': 'wB:p1', ...}}

    def run(*popenargs,
            input=None, capture_output=False, timeout=None, check=False, **kwargs):
        """Run command with arguments and return a CompletedProcess instance.
    
        The returned instance will have attributes args, returncode, stdout and
        stderr. By default, stdout and stderr are not captured, and those attributes
        will be None. Pass stdout=PIPE and/or stderr=PIPE in order to capture them,
        or pass capture_output=True to capture both.
    
        If check is True and the exit code was non-zero, it raises a
        CalledProcessError. The CalledProcessError object will have the return code
        in the returncode attribute, and output & stderr attributes if those streams
        were captured.
    
        If timeout (seconds) is given and the process takes too long,
         a TimeoutExpired exception will be raised.
    
        There is an optional argument "input", allowing you to
        pass bytes or a string to the subprocess's stdin.  If you use this argument
        you may not also use the Popen constructor's "stdin" argument, as
        it will be used internally.
    
        By default, all communication is in bytes, and therefore any "input" should
        be bytes, and the stdout and stderr will be bytes. If in text mode, any
        "input" should be a string, and stdout and stderr will be strings decoded
        according to locale encoding, or by "encoding" if set. Text mode is
        triggered by setting any of text, encoding, errors or universal_newlines.
    
        The other arguments are the same as for the Popen constructor.
        """
        if input is not None:
            if kwargs.get('stdin') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdin and input arguments may not both be used.')
            kwargs['stdin'] = PIPE
    
        if capture_output:
            if kwargs.get('stdout') is not None or kwargs.get('stderr') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdout and stderr arguments may not be used '
                                 'with capture_output.')
            kwargs['stdout'] = PIPE
            kwargs['stderr'] = PIPE
    
        with Popen(*popenargs, **kwargs) as process:
            try:
                stdout, stderr = process.communicate(input, timeout=timeout)
            except TimeoutExpired as exc:
                process.kill()
                if _mswindows:
                    # Windows accumulates the output in a single blocking
                    # read() call run on child threads, with the timeout
                    # being done in a join() on those threads.  communicate()
                    # _after_ kill() is required to collect that and add it
                    # to the exception.
                    exc.stdout, exc.stderr = process.communicate()
                else:
                    # POSIX _communicate already populated the output so
                    # far into the TimeoutExpired exception.
                    process.wait()
                raise
            except:  # Including KeyboardInterrupt, communicate handled that.
                process.kill()
                # We don't call process.wait() as .__exit__ does that for us.
                raise
            retcode = process.poll()
            if check and retcode:
>               raise CalledProcessError(retcode, process.args,
                                         output=stdout, stderr=stderr)
E               subprocess.CalledProcessError: Command '['bash', '<repo>/betrieb/einrichten.sh', '--erzeugen']' returned non-zero exit status 1.

    def run(*popenargs,
            input=None, capture_output=False, timeout=None, check=False, **kwargs):
        """Run command with arguments and return a CompletedProcess instance.
    
        The returned instance will have attributes args, returncode, stdout and
        stderr. By default, stdout and stderr are not captured, and those attributes
        will be None. Pass stdout=PIPE and/or stderr=PIPE in order to capture them,
        or pass capture_output=True to capture both.
    
        If check is True and the exit code was non-zero, it raises a
        CalledProcessError. The CalledProcessError object will have the return code
        in the returncode attribute, and output & stderr attributes if those streams
        were captured.
    
        If timeout (seconds) is given and the process takes too long,
         a TimeoutExpired exception will be raised.
    
        There is an optional argument "input", allowing you to
        pass bytes or a string to the subprocess's stdin.  If you use this argument
        you may not also use the Popen constructor's "stdin" argument, as
        it will be used internally.
    
        By default, all communication is in bytes, and therefore any "input" should
        be bytes, and the stdout and stderr will be bytes. If in text mode, any
        "input" should be a string, and stdout and stderr will be strings decoded
        according to locale encoding, or by "encoding" if set. Text mode is
        triggered by setting any of text, encoding, errors or universal_newlines.
    
        The other arguments are the same as for the Popen constructor.
        """
        if input is not None:
            if kwargs.get('stdin') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdin and input arguments may not both be used.')
            kwargs['stdin'] = PIPE
    
        if capture_output:
            if kwargs.get('stdout') is not None or kwargs.get('stderr') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdout and stderr arguments may not be used '
                                 'with capture_output.')
            kwargs['stdout'] = PIPE
            kwargs['stderr'] = PIPE
    
        with Popen(*popenargs, **kwargs) as process:
            try:
                stdout, stderr = process.communicate(input, timeout=timeout)
            except TimeoutExpired as exc:
                process.kill()
                if _mswindows:
                    # Windows accumulates the output in a single blocking
                    # read() call run on child threads, with the timeout
                    # being done in a join() on those threads.  communicate()
                    # _after_ kill() is required to collect that and add it
                    # to the exception.
                    exc.stdout, exc.stderr = process.communicate()
                else:
                    # POSIX _communicate already populated the output so
                    # far into the TimeoutExpired exception.
                    process.wait()
                raise
            except:  # Including KeyboardInterrupt, communicate handled that.
                process.kill()
                # We don't call process.wait() as .__exit__ does that for us.
                raise
            retcode = process.poll()
            if check and retcode:

tmp_path = PosixPath('/private/var/folders/9n/w5wl_q1x7j17v9y0b5nw6vj80000gn/T/pytest-of-<benutzer>/pytest-2713/popen-gw0/test_sandbox_nicht_in_erlaubni0')

    def test_sandbox_nicht_in_erlaubnisliste(tmp_path):
        """Sandbox- und Benutzerzeilen stehen in keiner Erlaubnisliste; fehlen sie in einer installierten Unit, ist das eine
        gesperrte Abweichung (Einmalschritt einrichten.sh --installieren), die units.py pruefen meldet."""
        helfer = (BETRIEB / "vorlagen" / "units-installieren.py").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
        # nur die Erlaubnisliste des Hilfsprogramms: Seine Prüfung „User= ist der Dienstbenutzer“ nennt "User" zu Recht
        helfer = helfer[helfer.index("ERLAUBT = {"):]
        helfer = helfer[:helfer.index("\n}\n")]
        for schluessel in ("ReadWritePaths", "ProtectSystem", "ProtectHome", "InaccessiblePaths", "User", "NoNewPrivileges"):
            assert not [k for k in units().ERLAUBT if schluessel in k[1]], schluessel
            assert f'"{schluessel}"' not in helfer, schluessel

    def run(*popenargs,
            input=None, capture_output=False, timeout=None, check=False, **kwargs):
        """Run command with arguments and return a CompletedProcess instance.
    
        The returned instance will have attributes args, returncode, stdout and
        stderr. By default, stdout and stderr are not captured, and those attributes
        will be None. Pass stdout=PIPE and/or stderr=PIPE in order to capture them,
        or pass capture_output=True to capture both.
    
        If check is True and the exit code was non-zero, it raises a
        CalledProcessError. The CalledProcessError object will have the return code
        in the returncode attribute, and output & stderr attributes if those streams
        were captured.
    
        If timeout (seconds) is given and the process takes too long,
         a TimeoutExpired exception will be raised.
    
        There is an optional argument "input", allowing you to
        pass bytes or a string to the subprocess's stdin.  If you use this argument
        you may not also use the Popen constructor's "stdin" argument, as
        it will be used internally.
    
        By default, all communication is in bytes, and therefore any "input" should
        be bytes, and the stdout and stderr will be bytes. If in text mode, any
        "input" should be a string, and stdout and stderr will be strings decoded
        according to locale encoding, or by "encoding" if set. Text mode is
        triggered by setting any of text, encoding, errors or universal_newlines.
    
        The other arguments are the same as for the Popen constructor.
        """
        if input is not None:
            if kwargs.get('stdin') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdin and input arguments may not both be used.')
            kwargs['stdin'] = PIPE
    
        if capture_output:
            if kwargs.get('stdout') is not None or kwargs.get('stderr') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdout and stderr arguments may not be used '
                                 'with capture_output.')
            kwargs['stdout'] = PIPE
            kwargs['stderr'] = PIPE
    
        with Popen(*popenargs, **kwargs) as process:
            try:
                stdout, stderr = process.communicate(input, timeout=timeout)
            except TimeoutExpired as exc:
                process.kill()
                if _mswindows:
                    # Windows accumulates the output in a single blocking
                    # read() call run on child threads, with the timeout
                    # being done in a join() on those threads.  communicate()
                    # _after_ kill() is required to collect that and add it
                    # to the exception.
                    exc.stdout, exc.stderr = process.communicate()
                else:
                    # POSIX _communicate already populated the output so
                    # far into the TimeoutExpired exception.
                    process.wait()
                raise
            except:  # Including KeyboardInterrupt, communicate handled that.
                process.kill()
                # We don't call process.wait() as .__exit__ does that for us.
                raise
            retcode = process.poll()
            if check and retcode:
>               raise CalledProcessError(retcode, process.args,
                                         output=stdout, stderr=stderr)
